She’s changed from her restrictive vinyl into practical, dark-grey leggings and a fitted black turtleneck, her twin tails of blonde hair secured with simple black ribbons. In one hand, she carries a small, insulated pouch. Her heart beats a nervous, exhilarating rhythm against her ribs.

She finds you waiting patiently, seated precisely where she’d instructed. A soft, adoring smile touches her lips as she approaches. "Daddy," she murmurs, her voice a conspiratorial whisper meant only for you.

Kneeling before you, she opens the pouch and retrieves three small objects, presenting them on her palm like precious jewels. The third is a miniature butterfly knot of deep, iridescent violet silk that shimmers like a nebula in the lunar glow.

"A gentleman must be properly attired," she whispers, her eyes dancing with mirth. With utmost care, she fastens the black tie to your stoic, central head. It sits perfectly, a mark of gravitas. Next, she affixes the gold knot to your leftmost head, the one that so often favors her with a happy pant. Lastly, she secures the violet silk to your rightmost head, the observant strategist of the trio.

Yet the enchantment holds. Miraculously, scaled to fit, the three bow ties remain perfectly knotted around the mighty throats of your three-headed Cerberus form. She taps the pouch with a paw, indicating it's spelled to stay put. "I brought us roast beef sandwiches," her telepathic voice projects into your minds, laced with playful humor. "Shall we?"

"We'd love to, Mrs. P., but unfortunately, we can't stay," Kim jumped in, grabbing Lucifer's sleeve and pulling him toward the door. "Super important... uh... alumni reception! Gotta fly! Literally!"

In one motion, she wrapped her arms around her mom's waist, burying her face in the familiar scent of antiseptic and hairspray. "Love you, Mom," she whispered, her voice thick with unshed tears. "Tell Dad and the boys I love them too."

Before her mother could respond, Kim released her, grabbed Lucifer's arm, and bolted for the door, calling over her shoulder, "Bye! Love you! Call you when I land!"

She exploded out the front door and onto the porch, gulping down lungfuls of suburban air as if she’d been drowning. Leaning heavily against the siding, she slid to the ground, her body wracked with silent, racking sobs. The five minutes were up.
